r1

=== BUGGY CODE ===
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])   
        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)
        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue
            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1
            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))
        return res

```python
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[1 + 1])   
        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)
        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue
            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1
            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))
        return res
```

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

Now, the code as fixed:
```
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])
        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)
        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue
            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1
            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))
        return res
```

But adding guard doesn't hurt? It might be considered extra but okay. However "output only fixed code" maybe no explanation. If add guard, need ensure imports. But if n=0, queries? Not needed. Could add:
```
        if n == 0:
            return [-1] * len(queries)
```
But then buildSparseTable not called. Is that acceptable? Yes. But maybe not necessary. Should we include? It changes behavior only for invalid input. Fine. But if problem constraints n>=1, no matter.

Let's think deeper: Is `bisect_left(a, [x, y])` truly correct for finding first index with nums1>=x? Suppose a sorted by nums1 then nums2. For query x,y, we need all indices k with nums1[k]>=x and nums2[k]>=y. If there are pairs with nums1 > x but nums2 < y before any valid, i points to first nums1 > x. But what if there is a pair with nums1 == x and nums2 >= y after some nums1 > x? Impossible because sorted by nums1, all nums1==x come before nums1>x. So if there is valid with nums1==x, i points to it before nums1>x. Good.

So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. If `getMax` accidentally accesses an unset entry due to `r - (1 << i) + 1` maybe > n-(1<<i)? We checked no. Let's formalize: For row i, valid j in [0, n - 2^i]. getMax uses j1 = l, j2 = r - 2^i + 1. Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. If `getMax` accidentally accesses an unset entry due to `r - (1 << i) + 1` maybe > n-(1<<i)? We checked no. Let's formalize: For row i, valid j in [0, n - 2^i]. getMax uses j1 = l, j2 = r - 2^i + 1. Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. If `getMax` accidentally accesses an unset entry due to `r - (1 << i) + 1` maybe > n-(1<<i)? We checked no. Let's formalize: For row i, valid j in [0, n - 2^i]. getMax uses j1 = l, j2 = r - 2^i + 1. Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. If `getMax` accidentally accesses an unset entry due to `r - (1 << i) + 1` maybe > n-(1<<i)? We checked no. Let's formalize: For row i, valid j in [0, n - 2^i]. getMax uses j1 = l, j2 = r - 2^i + 1. Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. So both j1 and j2 are <= n - 2^i. j1 = l <= r - 2^i + 1? Wait l <= r - 2^i + 1 because 2^i <= len => l <= r - 2^i + 1. So j1 <= j2 <= n - 2^i. Thus both set. Good.

Since l <= r - 2^i + 1? Actually interval length len = r-l+1 >= 2^i, so r - 2^i + 1 >= l. So j2 >= l >=0. Also j2 <= n - 2^i because r <= n-1 => r - 2^i + 1 <= n - 2^i. === RAW ANSWER ===
```python
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        if n == 0:
            return [-1] * len(queries)

        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])

        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)

        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue

            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1

            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))

        return res
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
import math
from typing import List
import bisect

class Solution:
    def maximumSumQueries(self, nums1: List[int], nums2: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        def buildSparseTable(arr, n):
            k = int(math.log2(n))
            st = [[None for j in range(n)] for i in range(k + 1)]
            for i in range(k + 1):
                for j in range(n - (1 << i) + 1):
                    if i == 0:
                        st[i][j] = arr[j]
                    else:
                        st[i][j] = max(st[i - 1][j], st[i - 1][j + (1 << (i - 1))])
            return st

        def getMax(st, l, r):
            i = int(math.log2(r - l + 1))
            return max(st[i][l], st[i][r - (1 << i) + 1])

        n = len(nums1)
        if n == 0:
            return [-1] * len(queries)

        a = sorted([[nums1[i], nums2[i]] for i in range(n)])
        max_y = [v[1] for v in a]
        for i in range(n - 2, -1, -1):
            max_y[i] = max(max_y[i], max_y[i + 1])

        _sum = [v[0] + v[1] for v in a]
        st_max_sum = buildSparseTable(_sum, n)

        res = []
        for x, y in queries:
            i = bisect.bisect_left(a, [x, y])
            if i == n or max_y[i] < y:
                res.append(-1)
                continue

            left, right = i, n - 1
            while left < right:
                m = (left + right + 1) // 2
                if max_y[m] >= y:
                    left = m
                else:
                    right = m - 1

            j = right
            res.append(getMax(st_max_sum, i, j))

        return res
